# Implementation log (newest entries first)

## 2026-08-27 (Claude: **The documentation speaks English**)

- **What:** the author's commission — "the testers and the people who look at this app closely are largely English-speaking". Translated: AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, SOUL.md, DESIGN.md, `docs/architecture.md` (was `architektur.md`), `docs/scenarios.md` (was `szenarien.md`), the complete backlog, and the implementation log from the F-Droid submission on. File names included; every path reference across the repo follows. No code change, therefore no version step.

- **The rule changed with it** (AGENTS.md §5): documentation and commit messages in English. Still German for now, and marked as such wherever they are referenced: the ADRs, the subject-matter concept, the root `README.md` (with `README.en.md` as its counterpart) and `docs/fdroid-anmeldung.md`.

- **Where the effort went, measured before deciding:** the six control and concept documents come to about 4,700 words together; the backlog is 38,800 and the log 36,900. So the two working documents are 90 % of the job. The backlog was translated in full — it is the living document, and its 29 open items are what a contributor reads to see what is known and planned. For the log, the author chose the cut: everything from 2026-08-17 (the F-Droid submission) on, which is the stretch that carries context for someone outside; the older history stays German behind a marker that says so. Structure checked rather than assumed: the backlog has the same 12 sections, 132 items and 29 open ones as before.

- **Three statements were corrected rather than translated as they stood**, because a freshly translated document that carries a known falsehood is worse than a German one: `architecture.md` still said the corpus audio was not versioned (wrong since ADR-0014), DESIGN.md still said the corpus is German (wrong since ADR-0016), and several backlog notes have since been overtaken by later work — those carry a bracketed pointer now instead of leaving the reader to find out.

- **Kept as they are:** the tags `[KLÄRUNG]` and `[PROP]` (identifiers the control files explain, not prose), the German `Screen`/class names in code references, and quoted German the author or a tester actually said.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` 261 cases / 0 failures — a docs batch cannot break the build, but the renames touched paths that code comments and `tools/generate_tts.py` refer to, so it was worth running. A grep over the repo finds no reference to the three old file names any more.

## 2026-08-27 (Claude: **The store description is accurate again — v0.36.3**)

- **What:** two sentences in `fastlane/.../full_description.txt` were out of date, in both languages: "it is in German" (wrong since v0.34.0) and "72 words and sentences in German" (since v0.35.0 there are 92, 20 of them English). Both corrected, plus a sentence that keeps the training language and the interface language apart. Version 0.36.2 → **0.36.3**, versionCode 47 → 48, changelog `48.txt` in both languages.

- **Why that gets a new number:** F-Droid reads the fastlane texts from the **pinned commit**. Slipping the correction in under 0.36.2 afterwards would mean two different shipped contents under one number — exactly what AGENTS.md DoD §6 forbids ("better one number too many than one that is ambiguous"). The tag `v0.36.2` therefore stays where it is; what gets built is `v0.36.3`.

- **The author corrected the MR description himself** while this batch was running: "Bilingual German/English interface, switchable on the start screen; the training corpus has its own language setting. Offline only, no internet permission." The copies in the repo (`fdroid/merge-request.md`, `-body.md`) still carried "German UI" and now match the live text word for word. The log entry of 2026-08-24 that listed the correction as open is marked as done — it had led me astray earlier today.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` 261 cases / 0 failures. No code change beyond the two version literals; the `check()` assertion in the build keeps both places identical.

## 2026-08-27 (Claude: **The crosstalk explained: the headset's microphone — v0.36.2**)

- **What:** the author tested with v0.36.1 on the A53 and brought back two things. First: with the hearing aid connected and a headset plugged in afterwards, the headset gets priority — which is exactly why the detection has asked about the routing rather than the connected devices since 2026-08-06, and the case is thereby confirmed a second time (ADR-0011 addendum). Second, and this is the actual finding: **"With a headset that has a mic I get a crosstalk effect. The selected channel is clearly louder, but the other one 'speaks' into the mic, and you hear that."** Version 0.36.1 → **0.36.2**, versionCode 46 → 47, changelog `47.txt` in both languages.

- **That settles the finding left open on 2026-08-06, and without any change to the audio path:** the crosstalk was "symmetrical and clearly quieter" — exactly the pattern of a microphone loop in the headset. The app sends exact zeroes on the silenced channel, unit-tested since v0.36.0. The ten-second test noted since August (tapping the headset microphone in silence) is settled without ever having become necessary. The spatializer trail from this morning is cleared with it: it was unlikely anyway (`isSpatialized=false`), and a second explanation is not needed.

- **What the app learns from it now stands in the channel test:** a calm line under the explanation — a headset with a microphone carries the playing side faintly over to the other one, and whoever wants a clean check uses headphones without a microphone. That is not decoration: for an outside tester it is precisely the difference between "the app does not separate" and "my headset is talking along". A document would have said it too, but nobody reads documents while judging a sound.

- **For chivalry's finding it still only works as an additional question, not as an explanation.** For the author the selected channel is "clearly louder" — so the direction is audible. chivalry reports no difference at all. If the microphone alone were at play, he would hear the direction. The detection hypothesis stays in front, and the diagnosis line from v0.36.1 remains the question that answers it.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` 261 cases / 0 failures. Proven on the desktop by screenshot (the new line sits under the explanation and the layout carries it without clipped edges). v0.36.2 is installed on the A53. The device screenshot stays open — the phone was locked for the whole session, and the author ran the listening checks himself.

## 2026-08-27 (Claude: **The detection now says what it sees — v0.36.1**)

- **What:** an addendum to the same day's version, triggered by chivalry's second report: "I tested the app on an older phone with 3.5mm jack. The issue just disappeared." The channel test now shows a line with the detected output setup **and** the reported device types in plain text. Version 0.36.0 → **0.36.1**, versionCode 45 → 46 (the patch place, because it is a tester finding after the bump, AGENTS.md DoD §6), changelog `46.txt` in both languages.

- **Why this and not the fix straight away:** the new report pushes the detection hypothesis to the front — a 3.5 mm jack reports `TYPE_WIRED_HEADSET`, which is in the table; USB-C reports, depending on the descriptor, `TYPE_USB_HEADSET` (22, in the table) **or** `TYPE_USB_DEVICE` (11, not in the table). That explains without strain why it fails on chivalry's USB-C headset and not on the author's. Only: which type **his** phone reports cannot be guessed from here, and he swapped two variables at once (the phone *and* the connector) — an older phone may also have no spatializer. Simply writing `TYPE_USB_DEVICE` into the table would therefore be a fix on suspicion, and one that softens ADR-0011's doubt rule on top. A screenshot of the new line ends the guesswork.

- **The line is built to survive a bug report:** "Output: stereo headphones detected · USB_HEADSET (22)" — the name **and** the constant, untranslated in both language versions because the names are Android's. Plus, when a hearing aid is detected, a second line: the channel selection in the settings then has no effect, **but this test still separates the ears**. The pair is the actual diagnosis: if the test separates cleanly while the line above says "hearing aid detected", the audio path is fine and the classification is wrong. This is shown only in the channel test — in the settings the verdict is the right amount, here it is a piece of evidence.

- **One query, not two:** `rememberOutputSetup` was extended into `rememberOutputDiagnosis` and returns the setup plus the device types; `rememberOutputSetup()` stays as a thin derivation. That means the types displayed and the decision the training screens pan by cannot diverge — a second query would be a second opinion.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` 261 cases / 0 failures (the reflection sweep `StringsCompletenessTest` covers the two new catalogue entries automatically). Proven on the desktop by screenshot, honestly reading "no devices reported" there (ADR-0011 point 6, no detection on the dev target). v0.36.1 is installed on the A53. **What stays open is the same as this morning:** the listening check on the device — the phone was locked for the whole test run, so the screenshot of the line with a real device type is outstanding too.

## 2026-08-27 (Claude: **The channel test bilingual, three words per ear — v0.36.0**)

- **What:** a commission from the author, triggered by the F-Droid tester chivalry: the channel test now speaks both languages and plays three words per ear instead of one; the raw microphone test has disappeared from that screen. Plus an analysis of the reported ineffective channel selection — without a code change, because the cause lies beyond our own hardware. Version 0.35.0 → **0.36.0**, versionCode 44 → 45 (both places), changelog `45.txt` in both languages.

- **Why the language exemption falls:** ADR-0015 had explicitly exempted the channel test — an instrument, reachable only through a long press on the version line. That reasoning carried as long as the only person who picks the instrument up reads German. Now the channel test is the **piece of evidence** for an English-speaking tester: it is the only place in the app that bypasses the setup detection and can therefore answer whether the app puts the sound where it says it does. A German instrument in English hands answers nothing. Access stays the long press, unchanged — being an instrument is decided by who finds it, not by which language it speaks (ADR-0015 addendum; AGENTS.md §5 and CLAUDE.md updated).

- **Three words instead of one:** a single word is over in half a second — too short to say with confidence which direction it came from, when that is precisely the open question. Both ears now get three **different** words simultaneously with a 350 ms gap between them; "which words do I hear" stays the criterion rather than "louder or quieter".

- **The arithmetic was pulled into `:core` and is thereby tested for the first time.** `buildTwoWordsPerEar` sat as an untested loop in `DevPlaybackScreen`; now `concatWithGaps` and `perEarStereo` stand in `core/audio/Mixer.kt` with seven new cases. The most important of them pins down the claim the whole screen exists for: with "left only" the right channel carries **exact zero**, not merely something quieter. Whatever is still audible there lies behind the app. Along the way, `ChannelMode → StereoGain` now lives once in `ChannelMixing.kt` and is read by the channel test **and** the settings — before that, the test could pass while the setting it stands in for meant something else.

- **The raw microphone test is gone, by the author's decision.** Its purpose was narrow and has been fulfilled since 2026-08-06: proving that the microphone path and the recording format are right on the real device before persistence builds on them. Recording productively has long gone through `RecorderControls`. On the channel test it was not a check but a distraction — the upper half of the screen, a shared `PlaybackQueue`, and a microphone permission request on a tool that only plays. That was the first thing an F-Droid tester saw there.

- **On the reported cause: nothing built in, because nothing is proven.** The whole path was read; the arithmetic is right. But the channel selection only takes effect if `applyChannelMode` detects the headphone setup, and only in learning and exam mode. Measured on the A53 (`dumpsys audio`): `mMasterMono=0`, `mMainBalance=0.0`, and a spatializer that is present and switchable per route (`mHasSpatializerEffect:true`, binaural and transaural) — the app's playbacks run there with `isSpatialized=false`, though, so for the A53 it is ruled out. Four hypotheses with their falsifications and three questions for the tester stand in the backlog (M9). The obvious intervention — `SPATIALIZATION_BEHAVIOR_NEVER` on the `AudioAttributes` — was checked against `android.jar` and exists (API 32, so with a guard at minSdk 29), but it sits there as a **prepared, not implemented** item: without a measurement from chivalry's device it would be a suspicion built in.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` 254 → **261** cases / 0 failures. Both language versions proven on the desktop by screenshot — the same build once with `-Duser.language=de` (the German corpus: Ball · Haus · Brot / Wasser · Tisch · Fenster), once with `en` and the English corpus (bread · cow · window / butterfly · umbrella · kitchen); the entry point temporarily set to `Screen.DevKanaltest` for the screenshot and reverted (checked with `git status`). v0.36.0 is installed on the A53 (`versionName=0.36.0`, `versionCode=45` confirmed via `dumpsys package`). **Open:** the listening check on the device — the A53 was locked during the test run, so a screenshot of the screen on the phone is outstanding.

## 2026-08-24 (Claude: **MR 46059 updated to 0.35.0**)

- **What:** `metadata/de.hexenwoche.audiolex.yml` in the fork raised to `0.35.0` / versionCode 44, the build entry `0.33.6` replaced rather than added (never shipped, and F-Droid would otherwise build it along to no purpose — and the test queue is long, according to linsui). Pushed to the branch `de.hexenwoche.audiolex`, and MR 46059 now points at `0e30de98`. No code change, therefore no version step. Pushed beforehand: the three open commits and an annotated tag `v0.35.0` on `c174d4ce`.

- **Why now, and why not a second MR:** the MR is open, not merged, and the app is not in the catalogue. So there is no automatic update path — the existing MR *is* the way. linsui asked for exactly that five days ago: "if you release a new version please update this MR."

- **The ten comments on the MR were the actual trigger** and not readable anonymously (API 401, already noted as a limit in the log of 17 August) — the author pasted them in. Their content: (1) linsui required a full commit hash instead of a tag/branch, long since done; (2) a static check by Andrew Pozdnakov with the result PASS (Apache-2.0, only `RECORD_AUDIO`, no `INTERNET`, no telemetry, the Piper tooling not part of the Android build); (3) linsui: "mostly ready", the test outstanding; (4) **chivalry 14 hours earlier: "I am not fluent at German. Could you please provide an English UI?"** That is exactly what came into being today — the tester is unblocked without having had to ask again.

- **The requirement observed:** `commit:` still carries a full 40-character hash, not a tag. The tag exists all the same, because `UpdateCheckMode: Tags` needs it for later automatic detection.

- **How it was verified before pushing:** the pinned commit built from a `git archive` export **without `local.properties`** — the way F-Droid's build server does it. `:composeApp:assembleRelease` green, 30.3 MB. Counted in the APK: 93 WAVs, 72 of them under `raw/de-DE/` and 20 under `raw/en-US/`, plus `bus.wav`; `words.json` and `recordings.json` both included. The recipe YAML parsed: the hash 40 hexadecimal characters, `versionName`/`versionCode` matching `CurrentVersion`/`CurrentVersionCode`.

- **Open and not in our hands:** a packager's test run. The MR description still claims "German language interface" — the sentence is outdated and belongs corrected; the text for it is with the author. *(Addendum 2026-08-27: done by the author.)* Only he can post comments, and there is no GitLab token here.

## 2026-08-24 (Claude: **The corpus by language, English examples — v0.35.0**)

- **What:** the author's second commission on the same day. The corpus gets qualified by language, and there are twenty English example entries. Version 0.34.0 → **0.35.0**, versionCode 43 → 44 (both places). The decision including the rejected alternatives: **ADR-0016**.

- **The construction comes from the author and is the heart of the matter:** the language is a **classification by whoever created the entry**, not a statement about its content. Whoever creates an entry picks the language; that only decides where it appears. If Andy records as German and mixes an English sentence in, that sentence stays under German. In his words: "it could be Chinese or Bushman, we could not prevent it anyway." That is also the only honest construction — the app sees the text only as a typed string, and the audio is opaque to it.

- **The model:** `CorpusLanguage { DEUTSCH, ENGLISCH }` in `core/corpus`, matched on the **primary subtag**, so `de-AT` → German. The shared helper `primaryLanguageSubtag` resolves the duplication `UiLanguage` had carried since that morning. `OwnEntry.language` as a BCP-47 string with the kotlinx default `"de-DE"` — a user file that a later version with more languages has to keep parsing. `AppSettings.corpusLanguage`, **DB v9 → v10** through `MIGRATION_9_10`.

- **Where the filter sits, and why that is not a matter of taste:** in `mergeCorpus`, **before** `kind` and `excludedSpeakers`. The corpus becomes SRS cards through `allOrSeed`. An English entry slipping through would sit as a card in the author's real deck until he deleted it by hand — which is why a foreign drawer drops out along with its recordings before anything else runs.

- **The UI language and the training language stay separate** (ADR-0015 had laid it out that way). Training German with an English interface is legitimate. Two settings in two places, with a line beneath the training language naming the difference — otherwise it is rightly confusing.

- **The voice choice of 2026-07-29 had to go.** Back then `en_US-lessac-medium` was set; that was before ADR-0014 and before F-Droid. Checked against the MODEL_CARDs: lessac is trained on Blizzard 2013, whose licence is limited to "research purposes" and explicitly excludes speech-synthesis products. `ryan` and `hfc_female`/`hfc_male` are CC BY-**NC**-SA — the same clause that got the three purchased noise loops thrown out in August — and `hfc` is fine-tuned from `lessac` on top. Chosen: **`en_US-ljspeech-high`**, a public-domain dataset (LibriVox recordings, book texts 1884–1964), the MODEL_CARD saying verbatim "trained from scratch", so it inherits nothing. The "high" tier rather than "medium" because of the kerstin finding from M1: the core exercise is the isolated single word, and low tiers compress there. **Measured** on the first output: "bread" 0.53 s, "cow" 0.50 s — against thorsten's "Ball" at 0.52 s. No compression.

- **Two pieces of legacy in the generator, both noticed while doing rather than while reading.** First, `generate_tts.py` rewrote `recordings.json` completely on every run and would thereby have **deleted the four hand-made human recordings** by Stephan and Grete — irreplaceable data that only still exists because nobody ran the script again before this batch. Second, the first run of the new version gave four German words that are deliberately spoken by humans only a synthetic thorsten duplicate. Both are now excluded in a data-driven way: hand-made entries are carried over, and a word with a real human recording gets no synthetic one. The script also only renders what is missing now (`--force` forces everything) — otherwise this batch would have rewritten 68 German WAVs for no reason.

- **A side finding in `.gitignore`:** `resources/sounds/*.wav` only reached one level deep. The TTS listening samples sat in a subfolder and would have landed in the repo on the next `git add -A` — exactly the accident ADR-0014 stands against. Now recursive, proven with `git check-ignore`.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` **254** cases / 0 failures. New among them `CorpusIntegrityTest`, the first test over the **shipped** corpus rather than over a fixture: every `fileRef` exists and decodes as 22050 Hz mono, every word has at least one recording, there are no orphaned recordings, and every language has content in both `kind` variants — the "a switch without content" lesson from the sentence arc, this time as a test rather than as an intention. Plus `CorpusLanguageTest`, `Migration9To10Test` and four settings cases. Checked visually on the desktop: the new section "Training language" with its hint line and the contingent list below it.

- **What stays open and is not claimed:** the listening check has so far only measured **duration**. Whether ljspeech is suitable for training is for the author's ear to decide — six files sit under `resources/sounds/hoerprobe-ljspeech/` for that (gitignored). The device test is outstanding too, for this batch including the migration v9 → v10. And there is no changing an existing entry's language afterwards: whoever files it wrongly creates it anew.

## 2026-08-24 (Claude: **The app speaks English — v0.34.0**)

- **What:** a commission from the author in two parts: localisation into English with the language choice on the entry screen, and a short guide, likewise reachable from the main page and following the language choice. Both implemented, plus a layout bug the first part made visible. Version 0.33.6 → **0.34.0**, versionCode 42 → 43 (both places), changelog `43.txt` in both languages.

- **Why now:** the app has been with F-Droid since 17 August, and the store entry has been bilingual since v0.33.0 — the app behind it was not. Whoever read the English description and installed it landed in a German interface. That makes the old rule "UI texts in German" (AGENTS.md §5, DESIGN.md, three times as a non-goal in the backlog) untrue; it is changed and the three backlog places are annotated. The decision including the rejected alternatives: **ADR-0015**.

- **No `strings.xml`, and this is the one place where it is worth the reasoning:** Compose Multiplatform 1.8.2 resolves the language of Compose Resources from the platform locale and offers no supported way to override it at runtime — a language choice *inside* the app would have worked against the framework. Instead the text catalogue is a typed interface: `core/i18n/Strings.kt` (around 150 entries), implemented by `GermanStrings` and `EnglishStrings`. A missing translation is thereby a compile error instead of an empty label on the device. The price is named: every new UI text costs three lines in three places, and the wording of the interface now lives in `:core` — unclean, but `:composeApp` has no test source set and `:core:jvmTest` is the fast loop.

- **The German texts were taken over verbatim.** No label changed; for a German user the switch is invisible. That was deliberate — it makes the diff checkable, and a before/after comparison on the device needs no feats of memory.

- **The language choice:** `AppSettings.uiLanguage` with `UiLanguage { SYSTEM, DEUTSCH, ENGLISCH }`, default `SYSTEM`. It resolves through the **primary subtag** — `de`, `de-AT`, `de_DE` give German, everything else English. The test machine runs on `de_AT`, so that was not an academic case. DB schema **v8 → v9** through `MIGRATION_8_9` (`ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`, the `MIGRATION_6_7` pattern); the destructive fallback explicitly does not carry it — SRS due dates and the session history have to survive the jump. Because the column defaults to `'SYSTEM'`, the update writes nothing and changes nothing on a German device.

- **The switch sits on the start screen, not in the settings.** Not out of convenience: whoever cannot read the current language must not have to guess which of five German buttons opens the settings. It still sits at the bottom in the quiet zone, each language written in itself ("Deutsch", "English"), the active one in the accent colour — so the row also answers "which language am I in right now". `SYSTEM` is deliberately not a selectable option, only the initial state.

- **The quick guide:** a new `Screen.Kurzanleitung`, the layout exactly the `ImpressumScreen` pattern (the title pinned, flowing text scrolling, "Back" pinned). Content: both modes, the five rating levels with their intervals and the explicit sentence that these are not grades, the four settings groups, own recordings, the backup. The text lives in the same catalogues as all labels — a second mechanism just for prose would be a second place where a translation can go missing.

- **The bug the batch uncovered:** with the language row and the quick guide, the start screen no longer fitted the default 800×600 desktop window — "Quit app", the imprint and the version line sat below the edge. Found in a screenshot, not guessed. The cause is older than this batch: `Spacer(Modifier.weight(1f))` anchors at the bottom but has no fallback; what does not fit is clipped rather than scrolled. That is the same class as the "Back" button that was unreachable three times. Replaced by `BoxWithConstraints` + `heightIn(min = viewport)` + `Arrangement.SpaceBetween` inside a scrolling column: anchored at the bottom while there is room, scrolling as soon as there is not. A weighted spacer cannot do that — inside a scrolling parent the available height is unbounded and its weight becomes zero. Recorded as a rule in DESIGN.md. On top of that, the desktop window now starts in phone proportions (440×900 dp): the dev target is meant to stand in for the A53, and a landscape format does not show what a phone shows.

- **Made language-free along the way:** `RecorderController` held its status as a finished German sentence ("Nimmt auf…"). It now holds a `RecorderStatus`, and only the display turns that into text. That was the only place outside a screen that decided on wording.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green. `:core:jvmTest` **231** cases / 0 failures, new among them: `UiLanguageTest` (subtag resolution including `de-AT`/`de_DE`/the empty string, the fallback to English, `resolve` never returning `SYSTEM`), `StringsTest` (enum mappings collision-free and non-empty, plurals distinguishing one from many, `joinLast`, interpolation carrying its value), `StringsCompletenessTest` (reflection over **all** interface members, both catalogues, every result non-empty), three cases for the new settings column and `Migration8To9Test`. The last is the **project's first migration test**: previous migrations were only checked on the device by pulling the database before and after — here `MIGRATION_8_9` runs against real SQLite on a hand-built v8 table and proves that the settings row keeps all seven values and that the new column arrives as `SYSTEM`. Deliberately without opening Room fully: rebuilding all three tables by hand just for Room's schema check would test the handwritten DDL rather than the migration. On the desktop: **the same build** once with `-Duser.language=de` and once with `en` — both start screens proven by screenshot, with the language switch showing the right language as active in each. The quick guide is proven in both languages by temporarily setting the entry screen to it and reverting afterwards (under Wayland nothing can be tapped on this machine, AGENTS.md §7).

- **What stays open and is not claimed:** the A53 test is outstanding — the migration v8 → v9 on a real database, switching by fingertip, and how the English labels wrap at phone width. The English version has not been read by a native speaker; for the imprint the German version stays the authoritative one.

## 2026-08-17 (Claude: **Submitted to F-Droid — fdroiddata!46059**)

- **What:** the merge request is up: [fdroiddata!46059](https://gitlab.com/fdroid/fdroiddata/-/merge_requests/46059), "New app: AudioLex", target `fdroid/fdroiddata:master`, one commit with one file (24 lines of recipe). Pipeline green, mergeable, no conflicts. All nine steps of the guide are thereby done; M6 has no open item left. No code change, therefore no version step — the version submitted is 0.33.6 / code 42.

- **Four stumbling blocks, every one of them stepped into on the first try** and now documented in step 9: the first pipeline failed **in the very millisecond it was created**, with zero jobs and no YAML error — that was GitLab's approval gate for new accounts, not the recipe; after confirming the account it ran. The "Run pipeline" form defaults to `master`, which, when forking a recipe repository, starts the check run over thousands of other people's apps instead of your own. The MR template overwrites a description already pasted in ("apply template"). And GitLab's rich-text editor is sluggish when pasting — switch to plain text.

- **What F-Droid's CI confirmed**, this time on their machines rather than in my container: `fdroid build` 316 s, `checkupdates` 175 s, `fdroid lint` 157 s, `rewritemeta`, `check source code`, `check apk`, `schema validation` — twelve jobs, all green. And the artefact log proves what the template explicitly asks for (a green build does not automatically mean a produced APK): `unsigned/: found 1 matching artifact files and directories`.

- **Two limits of remote observation**, so that the next session does not run into them: the job list and the comments of `fdroid/fdroiddata` are blocked anonymously (`401`/`404`). On the MR object itself, `head_pipeline.status`, `state`, `labels` and `user_notes_count` are readable — so it is visible *whether* someone commented, not *what*. In our own fork, by contrast, everything is open, and pipelines and jobs could be read in full there.

- **What did not land:** the command line `/label ~"New App"` from the template. It is no longer in the description, but no label was assigned — plausibly because only members of the target project may set labels. It cannot be proven for certain, since the system notes are blocked. Without consequence.

## 2026-08-17 (Claude: **F-Droid's update detection repaired — v0.33.6**)

- **What:** `fdroid checkupdates` reported "Couldn't find any version information". The build ran and the lint was clean — but the **automatic update detection** would have stayed blind: with `UpdateCheckMode: Tags`, every new version would have needed a merge request of its own, even though the recipe promises `AutoUpdateMode: Version`. The `fdroiddata` MR template lists auto-update under *strongly recommended*.

- **Why it failed, measured rather than guessed:** fdroidserver reads the version out of the Gradle file with two regexes — `\b[Vv]ersionCode\s*=?\s*["'(]*([0-9][0-9_]*)` and the counterpart for `versionName`. Both require a **literal** directly after the keyword; Gradle is not evaluated. In `composeApp/build.gradle.kts` it said `versionCode = appVersionCode` — a variable read out of `AppVersion.kt` by regex. So F-Droid found nothing.

- **How it was fixed (the author's decision out of three routes presented):** `versionCode = 42` and `versionName = "0.33.6"` now stand as literals in `defaultConfig`, with a detailed comment on why they **have to** stay literals. The duplicated maintenance only costs when it drifts apart — so a `check()` assertion prevents exactly that: it compares the literals with the values read from `AppVersion.kt` and aborts the build before an APK exists. **Proven**, not claimed: `versionCode` set to 99 as a test, and `assembleDebug` aborts with "Version drift: build.gradle.kts says 0.33.6 (99), AppVersion.kt says 0.33.6 (42)". The guarantee from DoD §6 (the displayed version = the built version) is thereby preserved, only now enforced rather than derived. AGENTS.md §6 and the header comment of `AppVersion.kt` are rewritten accordingly.

- **The store text pulled along:** `changelogs/41.txt` said "first release" — but versionCode 41 will never be published, and the recipe now points at `v0.33.6`. The file became `changelogs/42.txt` through `git mv`, in both languages; the content unchanged, the character counts still 398/388.

- **The recipe:** `versionName: 0.33.6`, `versionCode: 42`, `commit: v0.33.6`, `CurrentVersion`/`CurrentVersionCode` updated.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` with `--rerun-tasks` **210** cases / 0 failures. And the check that counts ran after the push: `fdroid checkupdates -v` against the real tag now says verbatim `..got package=de.hexenwoche.audiolex, version=0.33.6, vercode=42` instead of "Couldn't find any version information". The older tags still return `None` — they carry the variable version — which is without consequence, because F-Droid evaluates the newest one.

- **Reproducible builds: an author decision, F-Droid signs.** The data spoke for it (two byte-identical builds), three things against: the evidence comes from one machine, while F-Droid compares across several; the promise applies to every future version, not once; and a signing key of one's own would have to be kept safe for a lifetime. The price is named and accepted — the MR template says explicitly that it cannot be added later. In the MR text the box stands empty with the addition the template requires: "No, I don't want this."

- **A side finding from the MR template** (`.gitlab/merge_request_templates/App inclusion.md`, read in the cloned fork): the title has to follow the format "New app: app name", the fork has to be public and the branch **unprotected** (F-Droid merges by fast-forward), and on reproducible builds it says explicitly: *"if you don't enable reproducible build then the apk will be signed with our key so you can't enable it later"*. That is a one-way street and belongs decided before submitting — the data for it is in step 8 (two byte-identical builds).

## 2026-08-17 (Claude: **F-Droid deployment prepared — v0.33.0 → v0.33.5**)

- **What:** a commission from the author to prepare everything for inclusion in F-Droid, with two substantive requirements: work with **our own** WAVs (including recordings by him and Grete as examples), and **one background noise has to come along in any case** — his own bus recording ("not ideal, but legal"). Both correct point 3 of ADR-0014 and the first ADR-0010 addendum from that morning; both addenda are extended accordingly. Six of the nine steps from `docs/fdroid-anmeldung.md` are thereby done.

- **How (the admission conditions):** `LICENSE` applied — Debian's canonical Apache-2.0 text, unchanged except for the copyright line, proven by `diff` against the original. The toolchain-provisioning plugin is out of `settings.gradle.kts` (the whole `plugins { }` block), and `jvmToolchain(21)` resolves against the installed JDK 21. The 68 corpus WAVs (3.6 MB) are in the index, the `.gitignore` exception is dropped and replaced by a justification.

- **How (the noise catalogue returns):** the bundled branch that v0.32.0 had removed is back — `loadBundledNoiseScenarios` + two halves in the catalogue + branching on the `eigen-` prefix. What is new is not the code but the rule: only what may be redistributed gets bundled. The parsing now sits in `:core` (`parseNoiseCatalog`, defensive like `parseCorpus`), and only `Res.readBytes` stays in composeApp; the bundled half is thereby covered by `NoiseCatalogTest` (5 cases) **before** the first file exists. Until then `noise.json` is an empty array, the app starts with clean speech and says so in the settings.

- **How (the store and the application):** `fastlane/metadata/android/{de-DE,en-US}/` with a title, a short and a long description, a changelog text for versionCode 36 and the icon; all character limits counted (short description 76/79 of 80, long description 2525/2249 of 4000, changelog text 325/315 of 500 — with the day's addenda the texts grew to 2599/2309 and 398/388, measured again in the evening). The icon existed only as a vector drawable — ImageMagick's internal SVG renderer discarded the paths, so it was rasterised analytically (a dot plus three right-hand semicircular arcs with round caps, 4×4 supersampling, the inner 72 dp area of the 108 dp canvas). The recipe sits finished under `fdroid/metadata/de.hexenwoche.audiolex.yml` including a README that says it belongs in the `fdroiddata` fork and not here. The imprint: "without public distribution" is gone, and the README rewritten for strangers including a content licence table.

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` **210** cases / 0 failures (205 → 210 through `NoiseCatalogTest`). The real check was a different one: the **index** was exported with `git checkout-index -a --prefix` into an empty directory — without `local.properties`, without the author library, so the build server's situation — and built there with `ANDROID_HOME` from the environment. `assembleRelease --no-daemon` runs through, and the APK carries 68 corpus WAVs, `noise.json` and none of the old loops. That settles two assumptions from the guide: the desktop target part of the KMP module does not disturb the Android release build, and KSP/Room run through.

- **A second finding of the same kind as that morning:** after removing the toolchain plugin, its identifier still stood in the explanatory **comment** in the same file. F-Droid searches for it as text in Gradle files — the comment alone would have triggered the same scanner abort as the plugin. It now only appears in the documentation, which is not a Gradle file. A pattern worth filing: checking means checking the artefact, not the intention.

- **Open, exclusively with the author** (in detail in `docs/fdroid-anmeldung.md`, section "what only the author can do"): get the bus recording off the A53, four screenshots from the device, decide on a licence for the own recordings (proposal CC0-1.0), Grete's consent before checking in her voice, make the repository public and tag `v0.33.0`, the GitLab account and the merge request, acceptance on the hearing aid. What I can still contribute once the recording and the licence statement exist: put the sound in place, sort the screenshots in, run `fdroid lint`/`fdroid build` in the container.

- **Follow-up on the same day, two patch levels (DoD §6, changed after the bump):**

  - **v0.33.1 — the bundled background noise.** The author supplied the A53's pairing data; the bus recording ("Bus, inside", 2026-08-10) came off the device through `run-as` and sits **byte-identical** (SHA-256 compared) as `files/noise/bus.wav` in the repo. Nothing was edited, and that is justified by measurement: already 22050 Hz mono PCM16, the level flat across 5.6 s (±2.2 dB in 0.5 s windows, the first 2 s +0.07 dB against the average), RMS −19.4 dBFS / peak −1.9 dBFS — exactly what `loudnorm` produced for the old loops. The length suffices: the longest corpus entry lasts 3.76 s (`satz-zug`), so a wrap click cannot occur. **An open listening question:** the crest factor is 17.5 dB; at "advanced" (SNR −5 dB) the noise needs 3.1× gain, and 1.7 % of the samples then run into the mixer's clamp (at "hard", 0.035 %). Whether that is audible as crackle is for the ear to decide — the remedy would be a limiter pass, not a different SNR range.
  - **v0.33.2 — the demo human recordings and the author's decisions.** Four own recordings from the device into the bundled corpus (`stephan` 3, `grete` 1; 68 → **72** entries): the format fitted unchanged, the wording unchanged, only capitalisation and punctuation aligned with the existing stock. The author explicitly released them as **demo examples** and confirmed the **consent of the second speaker** — both recorded in `files/corpus/README.md`, which also says they can be switched off as a contingent of their own. The content licence set to **CC0-1.0** (author's decision), which removes the last open licence question. The imprint: only the e-mail address now, no phone number (author's decision). The store texts updated to 72 entries and the bundled sound. The `words.json` diff stays at 5 lines because the compact original style (one object per line) was restored instead of reformatting the file.

- **The desktop sight check, now actually documented:** the author corrected that the machine runs **natively on Debian** (GNOME/Wayland), not under WSL2 — the corresponding claims in AGENTS.md §7, CLAUDE.md, the README and `architecture.md` are corrected. That made the screenshot possible too: `gnome-screenshot` works (the X11 tools fail on Wayland, not on missing packages). The running app's window shows, under "Scenario", the entry **"Bus, inside" as selected**, background noise on, SNR +5 dB. So the bundled half of the catalogue is not only unit-tested but seen in the running app — including the resolution "empty/unknown id → the first entry". The listening check on the hearing aid stays with the author.

- **A rule updated:** AGENTS.md §5 had until then forbidden audio files in the repo ("versioning strategy open"). After the decision that was wrong and it is replaced: whatever ships belongs in the repo, on the conditions of redistribution rights and an origin line; third-party licensed material stays in `resources/sounds/`.

- **v0.33.3 — two findings from the device test that existed only there.** After installing 0.33.2 on the A53 (with the author's approval; the database, the WAL, the own corpus and the own noises pulled beforehand, and all of it present unchanged after the update), the contingent list showed **four** speakers instead of two: "Stephan" and "grete" from the device's own corpus next to "stephan" and "grete" from the newly bundled corpus — the same people, distinguished only by capitalisation. Fixed by naming the bundled voices `Stephan (Beispiel)` and `Grete (Beispiel)`: the list shows the `voiceId` verbatim, so the addition visibly separates the shipped demo from a real own voice — on every device, not just this one. File names and recording ids stay lean. **A second finding, not a bug:** "Bus, inside" appears twice in the scenario list, because the same recording still sits on the device as an *own* noise and is now bundled as well. On someone else's device that cannot occur; the author can delete his copy (one tap in "My background noises"), and then the list is unambiguous here too.

- **The store screenshots are done** (the point that stood on the author's list): four images, all from **one** build — **0.33.4**, read off the first image, which shows the version line (the log first said 0.33.3 here, which was wrong); all four files carry the same timestamp and the device clock on image 1 matches. Taken with `adb exec-out screencap` and navigated with `adb shell input tap` — the start screen, learning mode with a visible sentence, exam mode with a covered card, the settings with the training level. They sit under `fastlane/metadata/android/{de-DE,en-US}/images/phoneScreenshots/`. Two handles that were needed and will save time next time: `adb shell svc power stayon true` (the screen otherwise fell asleep mid-run and the result was a black always-on-display image) and afterwards `false` again.

- **v0.33.4 — the liability disclaimer and the duplicates really gone.**

  - **The imprint:** a new paragraph at the author's request — "use as is", without warranty and without any promise as to training effect, freedom from errors or data preservation, use at your own risk, no liability; with a note that the same is in the Apache-2.0 licence. The phone number was dropped in the same go (only the e-mail now, author's decision).
  - **The duplicates:** renaming to `(Beispiel)` had made them distinguishable, not removed them — on the A53 the same recordings sat twice, once bundled and once as own content from August. **Author's decision: delete the local copies.** Pulled in full beforehand (four recordings, the bus sound, `audiolex.db` + WAL: 74 cards, 3 sessions); then exactly what the app's delete path does — WAVs removed, the metadata set to `[]` — after checking in the code that `OwnCorpusRepository.delete`/`OwnNoiseRepository.delete` do nothing further (no card cleanup), so the file-level operation is equivalent. **Proven on the device:** "Bus, inside" now appears exactly once in the scenario list, and the contingent list starts with "Grete (Beispiel)" instead of a second "Grete".
  - **A side finding from the same sight check:** this time the hearing aid was connected — the output detection shows "hearing aid detected" and the channel selection correctly as ineffective with its reason (ADR-0011 point 5, seen in a picture for the first time in this session).
  - The four store screenshots were retaken at this state, so that the image set no longer shows duplicates. `svc power stayon` was reset afterwards.

- **The listening check on the hearing aid passed** (author, 2026-08-17): "very good". At "advanced" (SNR −5 dB) the bus sound is **very dominant** — which matches the setting, since the noise sits above the speech there — and easy to pull back with the slider. No mention of crackle: the measured clamping (1.7 % clipped samples at −5 dB) is not noticeable to the ear. That closes the open finding **without** a limiter pass; the file stays unedited, and the measurements are in `files/noise/README.md` so that a later crackle does not have to be measured again from scratch.

- **v0.33.5 — the app's origin in the imprint, and the worry behind it checked.** At the author's request, the imprint now says how the app came about: in interplay with Claude Code, "vibe-coded", with the division of labour (the idea, the subject-matter concept, the decisions and the acceptance with the author; a large part of the source from an AI) and the note that the ADRs and the journal in the open repository make that checkable. The author had added "in the hope that this does not prevent publication on F-Droid" — **checked rather than reassured:** F-Droid's inclusion policy does not mention AI-generated code; it is about licences, a free tool chain, proprietary blobs and buildability from source. So there is no rule the app could fail on, and the hopeful addition did not make it into the app's text. Classified in the guide (step 9): other projects (GCC, SDL) have adopted rules against AI contributions to their *own* codebases and an F-Droid rule is under discussion — that concerns upstreams accepting contributions, not a distributor; what is checked is today's policy. The recommendation there: raise the origin in the merge request itself.

- **Before making it public: the phone number out of the current state and the history** (no version step — nothing changes in the APK, `ImpressumScreen.kt` had already been cleaned in 0.33.2). The author's commission: commit, then clean the history. The audit before flipping the visibility found more than expected: the number stood not only in old commits but **also in the current state** — in `docs/backlog.md` (twice) and `docs/implementation-log.md`. The decision "publish only the e-mail address" had therefore only been half implemented. First the three text places were cleaned and the whole preparation state committed (120 files), then the history: the number stood in **40 of the 105 commits**, introduced with v0.16.0; replaced with `git filter-repo --replace-text` by `[Nummer entfernt]`. The tag `v0.33.5` sits locally at the head.

  **The evidence, in this order:** a complete mirror backup outside the repo before the intervention (15 MB, all refs including notes) · afterwards 0 hits on `master` and in the notes (`git rev-list` × `git grep`, all 105 commits) · the commit count unchanged at 105 · **the tree hash of HEAD identical to before** (`2be91e7`) — so the working state demonstrably did not change, only old blobs. Commit messages were never affected (checked separately: the hits on "430" were hash fragments, "Telefon" a word example from a Bluetooth finding).

  **Where hits still sit, and why that is right:** `refs/remotes/origin/*` points at the old, not yet overwritten state on GitHub — the 40 commits are unchanged there. Those refs came back unasked: filter-repo removes the `origin` remote deliberately, and as soon as it was entered again, VS Code's autofetch (15:06:05, documented in the reflog) pulled the old head once more. Not a regression, but the necessary qualification: `git rev-list --all` counts those refs and then reports 40 hits again. The intervention only reaches the outside with the push — until then the old version on GitHub is the valid one.

  **A finding that would have slipped through without looking:** `refs/notes/ai-attribution` (7 notes on AI attribution) hung on the old, no longer existing commit hashes after the rewrite. `git notes list` still showed them inconspicuously, and only `cat-file -e` said "dead" for all seven. Reattached through `.git/filter-repo/commit-map`, and all seven point at their commit again; the note contents themselves contained no hashes and stayed unchanged. For the next rewrite: filter-repo does not remap the note paths, that is manual work.

  **Why now and not later:** the intervention only works while the repository is private and nobody has cloned it — after that, clones and caches keep the old version. The recommendation to the author is in step 4 of the guide: delete the GitHub repository and create it anew rather than force-pushing, because GitHub keeps serving unreachable objects after a force push as long as you know the hash. Deliberately untouched: the two author names in the history (`bois1616` and `Stephan Reindl`, the same address) — the same person, not a private detail.

  **Two of my own errors in this entry corrected:** the screenshots come from build **0.33.4**, not 0.33.3 — read off image 1, which shows the version line. And the version line at the end still carried an older, contradictory addition ("four levels", "only 0.33.2 and 0.33.3 were ever on the device"); the state on the device is documented as 0.33.5 / code 41 via `dumpsys package`.

- **Public, and the build server's route walked through for real once.** The author pushed the cleaned state up and set the visibility to public. Two things to learn along the way: the **tag** push goes through even when the branch push bounces off the rewritten history — tags need no fast-forward. So after the first attempt the cleaned history including 8.17 MiB of objects was already on GitHub while `master` still pointed at the old state; only `--force-with-lease` pulled it along (`7f5cce1` → `754a339`). And: `--force-with-lease` needs an up-to-date remote-tracking ref for comparison, which `filter-repo` had just removed. The end state is documented with `git ls-remote` **without credentials** (`credential.helper` empty, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`): `master`, `v0.33.5` and the notes point at `754a339`, readable anonymously.

  **Which makes check 1 from step 7 available in its strict variant:** `git clone --branch v0.33.5 --depth 1` from GitHub into an empty directory, no `local.properties`, `assembleRelease --no-daemon` — green. Not stopping at the build but looking at the artefact: `aapt2 dump badging` gives versionCode **41**, versionName **0.33.5**, minSdk 29, compileSdk 35 — the same values the recipe expects. The APK contains 72 corpus WAVs, `bus.wav` at 247,004 bytes (byte-identical to the device recording) and `noise.json`; assets 4.3 MB, the APK 29 MB. **A side finding:** Compose Resources packs the two origin READMEs in as well (10 KB) — no harm, but those texts ship, and it is worth knowing that before writing anything internal into such a README.

  **Documentation drift collected.** The sample store texts in the guide still claimed the app ships no background noise and named a changelog text for versionCode 35 — they are replaced by a table with the measured character counts of the eight real files. Two versions of the same text drift apart; here it was demonstrated, so the copy is gone rather than maintained. The character counts were outdated too (long description 2599/2309 instead of 2525/2249, changelog text 398/388 instead of 325/315), because the texts grew after the measurement. The section "what only the author can do" has shrunk from three points to **one**: the GitLab account, the fork, the merge request. The hearing-aid acceptance is done ("the hearing test is very good", the sound dominant at "advanced" but correctable with the slider, no crackle) — which closes the measurement finding about clamping, and `bus.wav` stays unedited.

- **Running F-Droid's own tools over the recipe (check 2, step 7) — and a finding along the way that goes beyond the check.** Image `registry.gitlab.com/fdroid/fdroidserver:buildserver` (2.37 GB), fdroidserver 2.4.5. The result: `readmeta` ok, **`lint` return code 0** without a complaint, `rewritemeta` with no difference (the recipe is canonically formatted), **the scanner with no finding** — it would otherwise have aborted the build — and `fdroid build -v -l` with `1 build succeeded` in 1m 29s, producing `de.hexenwoche.audiolex_41.apk` (30,400,285 bytes) plus a source tarball. That answers the three open questions from step 7: Gradle 8.11.1 is available (fdroidserver downloads it and checks the checksum), the scanner does not trip anywhere, and the build needs no special handling in the recipe. Learned along the way: fdroidserver **removes** `gradle-wrapper.jar` and uses its own Gradle — the precompiled jar in the repo is not an obstacle to inclusion.

  **The APK from the container is byte-identical to the one from check 1** — the same 30,400,285 bytes, the same SHA-256, even though one was built with this machine's SDK and the other with F-Droid's SDK and their downloaded Gradle. That is the technical precondition for reproducible builds with one's own key. The recommendation nevertheless stays to let F-Droid sign the first release: the evidence comes from **one** machine with one kernel and one JDK major version, and a first submission with a reproducibility promise has more moving parts than necessary. The finding is in step 8, so that the decision later rests on numbers.

  **Four pitfalls in the setup, every one stepped into individually** and now documented in step 7, because the commands that stood there before would have failed at each of them: the image does **not** contain fdroidserver (a pure build environment, with the checkout mounted in) and `fdroid` is not on the `PATH`. `ANDROID_HOME` is only in the profile, so `bash -lc` rather than `bash -c`. The data directory has to be a **git repository**, or the `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` fallback returns `None` and the run dies with `TypeError: str expected, not NoneType` — I first took that for an fdroidserver bug and had to correct the diagnosis; 2.4.5 and `master` behave the same here. And `fdroid lint` checks categories against `config/categories.yml` including category icons from `fdroiddata`; if those are missing, *every* category is invalid. That was also the reason for the first false alarm: the 108-entry list from `fdroiddata` contains both `Science & Education` and `Sports & Health`.

  **Two corrections to my own statements the same day:** issues on the GitHub repo are **enabled** — the start page carries `id="issues-tab"`, which GitHub only renders with issues enabled. The 404 on `/issues` that I reported twice comes from this environment's HTTP proxy (the same proxy answers `api.github.com` with 504). And the categories were never wrong; my local advance check had validated against a list built from memory instead of against the real one.

- **Version:** 0.32.0 → 0.33.0 → 0.33.1 → 0.33.2 → 0.33.3 → 0.33.4 → **0.33.5**. Six steps in one day; each has a visible difference in the artefact (admission conditions · the bus sound · demo human recordings and author decisions · the contingent finding from the device · the disclaimer and clearing the duplicates · the origin note). On the device 0.33.2 through 0.33.5 ran; the state that evening is documented via `dumpsys package` (versionName 0.33.5, versionCode 41).

## 2026-08-17 (Claude: **Bundled background noises removed, the F-Droid application worked out — v0.32.0**)

- **What:** two commissioned pieces of release work (ADR-0014 point 4 thereby partly starts; the publication itself stays uncommissioned). First: the three licence-bound loops (salamisound ×2 "non-commercial", Pixabay ×1) are out of the project — the goal was "no external licence dependency at all in the shipped state", not merely "no longer referenced". Second: `docs/fdroid-anmeldung.md`, the step-by-step guide to inclusion in F-Droid including deliverables, documents and text templates.

- **How (assets):** `files/noise/` is gone — `noise.json` and `README.md` (the licence table) removed from versioning, the three WAVs moved into the gitignored author library `resources/sounds/` next to their MP3 sources. **Nothing deleted**: the loops stay available for private import on the device, they are just no longer shipped. The `.gitignore` patterns for `files/noise/` stay as a barrier (with the reasoning in a comment), so that a file placed there does not accidentally travel into a release.

- **How (code):** `loadNoiseScenarios` (the bundled branch, `Res.readBytes`) deleted; `loadAllNoiseScenarios` now reads only the own noises; `loadNoiseBuffer` has no branch any more, and every WAV comes from `OwnNoiseRepository`. `NoiseScenario` lost `source`/`license` and is no longer `@Serializable` — both fields carried the origin of foreign loops, and without `noise.json` the type is deserialised nowhere. The id prefix `eigen-` stays: the ids are persisted, and renaming would have silently discarded the stored selection on every existing installation. `AppSettings.noiseScenario` has no default any more (empty = "none chosen", and the blank guard in the mapper goes with it); only the Kotlin default changed, not the column — **no** schema change, the DB stays at version 8. New in the UI: with an empty catalogue, the line "No sound yet — playback stays clean." stands under "Scenario" instead of an empty radio group (the published normal case per ADR-0014).

- **How it was verified:** `./gradlew build` green, `:core:jvmTest` **205** cases, 0 failures (204 → 205: one new case for stored old ids, two existing cases switched to the new default semantics). Evidence in the artefact rather than a claim: `unzip -l` on the debug APK shows **0** files under `files/noise/` and 71 resource files (the corpus). The desktop target starts with this state (the process ran until I aborted it, no exception in the log). **A side finding that almost slipped through:** the first `assembleDebug` after the deletion still carried the loops **in the APK** — `copyDebugComposeResourcesToAndroidAssets` does not clean up removed resources, and the files were still lying in `build/intermediates/assets/`. Only after `:composeApp:clean` was the artefact clean. After removing a Compose resource, a clean is therefore mandatory, or you are checking the past.

- **Open (author):** a sight check of the empty line in the settings and a listening check "background noise on, no own sound → clean audio" (a desktop screenshot is not possible under WSLg, X access to the window is missing). On the A53 additionally: the installation there has `restaurant` stored; after the update the selection has to heal onto the first own sound, or show the new line with an empty catalogue — exactly the migration path the two new tests cover at the mapper level.

- **How (the F-Droid guide):** nine steps from "apply the licence" to "merge request", each with a deliverable and a check command; plus the document list (LICENSE, README, fastlane metadata, the privacy text, `metadata/de.hexenwoche.audiolex.yml`) with finished text templates and the source list. All of it proven against the current F-Droid documentation, as the evidence obligation requires. **Three findings** that fell out of it and stand as backlog items: (1) the Foojay toolchain plugin in `settings.gradle.kts` makes F-Droid's scanner abort — a real blocker nobody had on the list; (2) "TTS in the build" from ADR-0014 does not carry, because the Piper voice model from Hugging Face is not a permitted binary source — the way out, "generate locally, check in", needs an author decision (`[KLÄRUNG]`); (3) the licence question of the voice is answered and uncritical (the model MIT, the dataset CC0).

- **Version:** 0.31.1 → **0.32.0**.

---

**The entries below are still in German.** They cover 2026-07-07 to 2026-08-07 — the scaffolding, the audio work, exam mode and SRS, the own corpus, the backup, up to the release preparation. Documentation switched to English on 2026-08-27 (AGENTS.md §5); everything from the F-Droid submission on is translated above, and the earlier history was left as it was rather than retold. Where those entries matter for a decision, the backlog and the ADR concerned carry the substance in the language they are written in.

## 2026-08-07 (Qwen: **Szenario-Auswahl heilt gelöschte eigene Geräusche — v0.31.1**)

- **Was:** A53-Abnahme-Befund zu v0.31.0: Nach dem Löschen des ausgewählten eigenen Geräuschs zeigte die Szenario-Auswahl in den Einstellungen „nichts" (kein Radio aktiv, auch Ein/Aus-Toggeln heilte es nicht), während die Wiedergabe still auf den ersten Katalogeintrag zurückfiel — Anzeige und Verhalten liefen auseinander. Die Schärfung (AC2) hatte den Wiedergabe-Fallback festgelegt, die UI-Anzeige aber nicht bedacht. **Autor-Entscheid 2026-08-07: beim Öffnen heilen.**
- **Wie:** Im Katalog-Laden des `EinstellungenScreen`: existiert die gespeicherte Id nicht mehr und der Katalog ist nicht leer, wird das erste verfügbare Szenario übernommen und gespeichert. Ein leerer Katalog (Release-Fall, ADR-0014) bleibt ungeheilt — es gibt nichts zu übernehmen, und der ADR-0010-Fallback (saubere Sprache) deckt die Wiedergabe.
- **Wie verifiziert:** `./gradlew build` grün. **A53-Probe steht aus:** ausgewähltes eigenes Geräusch löschen → Einstellungen öffnen → die Auswahl muss sichtbar auf das erste Szenario geheilt sein. Import-/Sicherungs-Proben der v0.31.0-Abnahme laufen parallel weiter.
- **Version:** 0.31.0 → **0.31.1** (Patch, DoD §6).

## 2026-08-07 (Qwen: **Eigene Störgeräusche: aufnehmen, importieren, löschen — v0.31.0**)

- **Was:** Das M4-Item „Eigene Störgeräusche", am selben Tag geschärft und als `[→Sonnet]` freigegeben (plus zweiter Nachtrag zu ADR-0013). Eigene Geräusche werden zweite, schreibbare Quelle neben dem gebündelten Katalog: App-Verzeichnis `eigene-stoergeraeusche/` mit `geraeusche.json` + WAVs (Muster Eigen-Korpus, keine Datenbank); der zusammengeführte Katalog bedient Einstellungen und `loadNoiseBuffer`; der Import validiert WAVs strikt (22050 Hz, mono, PCM16) und lehnt ab statt zu konvertieren; die Sicherung bekommt die dritte Inhaltsart (`stoergeraeusche/`, rein additiv, `format = 1` bleibt). Ändern ist kein Ziel (Autor-Entscheid): löschen und neu aufnehmen. **Arbeitsteilung:** Schärfung, ADR-Nachtrag, Diff-Review und DoD in der Hauptsession; die Umsetzung lief delegiert über einen Subagenten (zweiter Delegations-Batch).

- **Wie (`:core`):** `OwnNoise`-Modell + `OwnNoiseStore` (defensiver Parse; strenge Variante für das Backup), `checkOwnNoiseImport` über `WavFile.decode` (sealed-Votum Ok/NotAWav/WrongFormat), `OwnCorpusBackup` mit drittem Ordner (kein Ordner ohne Inhalt, fehlend ≠ beschädigt, Merge über id). 23 neue Tests: Store-Roundtrip + Beschädigung (8), Import-Validierung (7), Backup mit Geräuschen, Altarchiv ohne Ordner und beschädigte Metadaten (+8 in `OwnCorpusBackupTest`/`ZipArchiveTest`).

- **Wie (composeApp):** `OwnNoiseRepository` + `OwnNoiseFiles` (Android-/Desktop-Actuals); neuer Screen „Eigene Störgeräusche" (Sackgasse, erreichbar aus der Störgeräusch-Sektion der Einstellungen) mit Aufnahmemaske — der Rekorder wurde als `RecorderControls.kt` aus der Eigen-Korpus-Maske herausgelöst — inkl. Loop-Hinweis, Import über `BackupActions.pickWav` (Muster `pickArchive`); zweiquelliger Katalog in `NoiseMixing.kt` (eigene ids `eigen-<id>`; die Leer-/Fehlfallbacks aus ADR-0010 Punkt 4 unverändert); `Backup.kt` koordiniert jetzt drei Quellen, Export-/Import-Meldungen nennen Geräusche getrennt und schweigen bei Abwesenheit.

- **Wie verifiziert:** `./gradlew build` grün (inkl. `lintDebug`, 0 Issues); `:core:jvmTest` 181 → **204** (23 neue Fälle, 0 Fehler) — von der Hauptsession selbst nachgefahren. **Offen:** A53-Abnahme (AC6): echtes Geräusch aufnehmen + Loop-Probe im Training, Import einer passenden und einer unpassenden Datei, Löschen samt Szenario-Fallback, Sicherungsrunde mit Geräuschen; Desktop-Bedienprobe. Merksatz für den Autor-Blick: Der Einstiegs-Button in den Einstellungen sitzt unter der Szenario-Auswahl und ist damit nur bei eingeschaltetem Störgeräusch sichtbar (wörtliche Lesart von AC3).

- **Version:** 0.30.0 → **0.31.0**.

## 2026-08-07 (Qwen: **Szenario-Presets Einfach/Schwierig/Fortgeschritten — v0.30.0**)

- **Was:** Das M4-Item „Szenario-Presets" (S9), am selben Tag geschärft und als `[→Sonnet]` freigegeben. Ein-Tipp-Trainingsstufen: Ein Preset schreibt die **atomaren** Werte (`noiseEnabled`/`snrDb`), die Stufe selbst wird **nicht** persistiert — welche Stufe gerade gilt (oder keine), wird je Rekompilation aus den atomaren Werten abgeleitet. Stufen: Einfach = Störgeräusch aus (SNR unverändert), Schwierig = an bei +5 dB (hörgeprüfter Default), Fortgeschritten = an bei −5 dB (anderer Regler-Anschlag); `noiseScenario` bleibt bewusst preset-neutral (Loops lautheitsnormalisiert seit v0.13.0, Schwierigkeit kommt allein über den SNR). Keine Spalte, keine Migration, `SettingsEntity` unangetastet. **Arbeitsteilung:** Schärfung, Diff-Review und DoD in der Hauptsession; die Umsetzung lief delegiert über einen Subagenten (autorisiert, vorerst dasselbe Modell). Randnotiz: Der erste Delegationslauf starb mitten in der Umsetzung an einem externen Quotenlimit und hinterließ einen nicht kompilierbaren Teilstaat; der zweite Lauf hat ihn vorgefunden, gegen die ACs geprüft und vervollständigt.

- **Wie (AC1, `:core`):** `SettingsProfile`-Enum in `core/settings` mit den Stufen-Parametern im Konstruktor als **einziger** Definition; `applyProfile`/`derivedProfile` lesen nur diese Tabelle, darum können Anwendung und Ableitung nicht auseinanderlaufen. `SettingsProfileTest` mit 9 Fällen: Anwendung je Stufe (EINFACH lässt `snrDb` über die ganze Spannweite unangetastet), alles außer dem Noise-Paar preset-neutral, Ableitung inkl. Randfälle (aus + beliebiger SNR = Einfach; an + jeder andere SNR = keine Stufe), Apply/Derive-Roundtrip.

- **Wie (AC2/AC3, composeApp):** `App.kt` leitet `activeProfile = settings.derivedProfile()` ab und schreibt über `updateSettings { it.applyProfile(profile) }` — derselbe Pfad wie eine manuelle Änderung. `EinstellungenScreen`: neue **erste** Sektion „Trainingsstufe" (über „Erscheinungsbild", `RadioOption`/`selectableGroup`), Labels exakt „Einfach"/„Schwierig"/„Fortgeschritten", bei keiner passenden Stufe keine aktive Option und die Zeile „Individuell eingestellt" (`labelSmall`, `onSurfaceVariant`).

- **Wie verifiziert:** `./gradlew build` grün; `:core:jvmTest` 172 → **181** (9 neue Fälle, 0 Fehler) — von der Hauptsession selbst nachgefahren, nicht aus dem Subagenten-Bericht übernommen; `:composeApp:assembleDebug` grün. **A53-Bedienprobe (AC4) am 2026-08-07 mit v0.30.0 bestätigt:** „Schwierig" → Schalter an und Regler auf +5; Regler manuell verstellen → Auswahl entfällt, „Individuell eingestellt" erscheint; „Einfach" → Störgeräusch aus. Angenommener Nebenbefund: Die „Individuell"-Zeile ist nur bei aktivem Störgeräusch sichtbar — bei ausgeschaltetem greift die Ableitung zurück auf „Einfach"; vom Autor ausdrücklich in Ordnung befunden. Dieselbe Sichtprobe hat die noch offene `FlowRow`-Frage des Kanaltests (v0.28.1) mit abgeräumt: Die drei Kanaltasten brechen sauber um.

- **Version:** 0.29.1 → **0.30.0**.

## 2026-08-07 (Opus: **Sitzungshistorie wird mitgesichert — v0.29.1**)

- **Abnahme am A53 (2026-08-07): bestanden, voller Durchlauf mit Deinstallation.** Und diesmal **mit vollständiger Sicherheitskopie vorab** — Datenbank samt WAL **und** Eigen-Korpus per `run-as` gezogen, bevor irgendetwas angefasst wurde. Genau das war beim vorigen Durchlauf versäumt worden und hat die Historie gekostet. Gemessen:

  - Export schrieb `Documents/audiolex-sicherung-2026-08-07-1345.zip` mit **`sitzungen/verlauf.json`** neben `eigene-aufnahmen/`. Inhalt heruntergezogen und geöffnet: eine Sitzung, `startedAtEpochMillis` 1786090371423, `PRUEFMODUS`, 15 Bewertungen (3 AGAIN, 7 SOON, 5 LATER).
  - Nach Deinstallation und Neuinstallation: leerer Korpus, `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM SessionEntity` = **0**, „Exportieren" korrekt ausgegraut.
  - Nach dem Import: 4 Aufnahmen zurück **und** die Sitzung mit exakt denselben Werten (`1786090371423|PRUEFMODUS|15|3|7|5|0|0`) — an der Datenbank nachgesehen, nicht am Bildschirm.
  - Dieselbe Datei ein zweites Mal importiert: „Nichts hinzugefügt — 4 Eintrag/Einträge und 1 Sitzung(en) der Sicherung sind bereits vorhanden." Sitzungszahl blieb 1, Aufnahmen blieben 4. Die Zusammenführung über `startedAtEpochMillis` hält am Gerät (AC3).

- **Was:** Korrigiert **ADR-0013 Entscheidung 6**, deren Begründung sich als zur Hälfte falsch erwiesen hat. Sie fasste SRS-Fälligkeiten und Sitzungshistorie unter „Verlaufsdaten, die sich neu bilden" — richtig für die Fälligkeiten, falsch für die Historie. Die bildet sich gerade nicht neu: Sie ist die Aufzeichnung dessen, was geschehen ist, und bei einem Training über Monate die einzige Größe, an der sich Fortschritt ablesen lässt. Aufgefallen ist der Fehler erst, als der AC8-Test die Daten wirklich löschte und der Autor nachfragte. Das Wort „Verlaufsdaten" hat ihn getragen — es klingt nach „vorübergehend" und meint hier das Gegenteil.

- **Wie (AC1/AC2, `:core`):** `Session` ist jetzt `@Serializable` (Präzedenz `OwnEntry`), dazu `encodeSessions`/`parseSessionsOrNull`/`sessionsToAdd` in neuem `core/session/SessionStore.kt`. Das Archiv bekommt `sitzungen/verlauf.json` als zweite Inhaltsart — **rein additiv**: Die Formatmarke bleibt `format = 1`, und ein Archiv **ohne** `sitzungen/`-Ordner bleibt gültig (fehlende Datei → leere Liste). Das war keine Nachsicht, sondern Pflicht: Die drei Sicherungen, die heute Vormittag entstanden sind, haben keinen solchen Ordner und liegen bereits im Dokumentenverzeichnis des Autors.

  **Die Unterscheidung, die dabei zählt:** *fehlende* Historie ist normal, *vorhandene aber beschädigte* macht das ganze Archiv unlesbar. Andernfalls hätte ein kaputter Verlauf als „0 Sitzungen übernommen" gemeldet — dieselbe stille Falschmeldung, gegen die es `parseOwnCorpusOrNull` schon gibt.

- **Wie (AC3, Zusammenführung):** Identität ist `startedAtEpochMillis`, **nicht** `SessionEntity.id` — die ist `autoGenerate` und damit gerätelokal; auf zwei Geräten trägt dieselbe Zahl verschiedene Sitzungen. Ein Test hält das ausdrücklich fest (zwei Sitzungen mit gleichem Startzeitpunkt, aber verschiedenen Zählern, gelten als bekannt).

- **Wie (AC4, Verdrahtung):** Neues `composeApp/Backup.kt` koordiniert die beiden Quellen. `OwnCorpusRepository` bekommt **keine** `SessionRepository` — es nimmt die Sitzungen als schlichte Liste entgegen und gibt mit `applyImport` nur seine eigene Hälfte zurück. Die eine Stelle, die beides kennt, ist die neue Datei. `sessionRepository` ist in `App.kt` aus zwei Aufrufstellen herausgezogen, die es bis dahin je einzeln bauten (zwei Instanzen über einem DAO).

- **Wie (AC5/AC6, Texte):** Meldungen nennen beide Inhaltsarten getrennt und schweigen zu der, die im Archiv nicht vorkommt — ein Altbestand ohne `sitzungen/` darf nicht als „0 Sitzungen" erscheinen. Impressum **und** der Hinweis über den Schaltflächen sind nachgezogen: Beide sagten „eigene Aufnahmen", der Export enthält jetzt mehr als der Text zusagt.

- **Wie verifiziert:** `./gradlew build` grün, `:core:jvmTest` 161 → **172** (11 neue Fälle: Roundtrip mit Sitzungen, Merge über den Startzeitpunkt, Identität *nicht* über die Room-id, Altarchiv ohne `sitzungen/` importierbar, beschädigte Historie → unlesbar, leere ≠ beschädigte Historie, unbekannter Bewertungsschlüssel bleibt lesbar). Gerätetest wie oben. Version 0.28.1 → 0.29.0 → **0.29.1**; der Patch-Schritt, weil der Hinweistext über den Schaltflächen erst nach der Installation von 0.29.0 nachgezogen wurde.

## 2026-08-07 (Opus: **Kanaltest per Langdruck auf die Versionszeile — v0.28.1**)

- **Vorgeschichte, die hier hingehört: v0.27.0 war an der Spezifikation vorbeigebaut.** Für den Kanaltest gab es seit dem 2026-08-06 ein **geschärftes Backlog-Item mit AC1–AC3 und Nicht-Zielen** — ich habe es vor der Umsetzung nicht gelesen (AGENTS.md §2 verlangt genau das) und stattdessen eine eigene Lösung über `BuildConfig.DEBUG` gebaut. Die verstößt gegen ein ausdrückliches Nicht-Ziel („keine Debug-/Release-Unterscheidung über BuildConfig"), erfüllt AC1 nicht (verlangt war die Langdruck-Geste) und ließ AC2 ganz aus. Der Unterschied war nicht kosmetisch: Bei meiner Fassung wäre das Werkzeug aus Release-Builds verschwunden, statt in jedem Build erreichbar zu bleiben. **Autor-Entscheid 2026-08-07: wie geschärft umsetzen.** Das BuildConfig-Gating ist vollständig zurückgebaut (`DebugBuild.kt` samt Actuals gelöscht, `buildFeatures { buildConfig = true }` wieder raus).

- **Wie (AC1):** Der „Kanaltest (Dev)"-Knopf ist vom StartScreen verschwunden. Der Screen öffnet sich über **langes Drücken auf die Versionszeile** (`Modifier.combinedClickable(onLongClick = …, onClick = {})`), bewusst ohne Ripple und ohne sichtbaren Hinweis — eine erkennbare Affordanz würde das Entwicklerwerkzeug genau dorthin zurückholen, wo es dieses Item entfernt. `Screen.DevKanaltest` und `DevPlaybackScreen` bleiben verdrahtet.

  **API vorab gegen die echten Jars geprüft** (AC1 verlangt es, Präzedenz `FlowRow`/`TextAutoSize`/`Slider`): `combinedClickable` liegt in `foundation-desktop-1.8.2.jar` in vier Überladungen vor. Die im AC vermutete `@OptIn`-Anforderung besteht **nicht** — der Compiler verlangt keine, die Funktion ist in CMP 1.8.2 stabil.

- **Wie (AC2):** Die Wortliste im `DevPlaybackScreen` zeigt statt aller 58 Einträge nur noch die zwei, die der Kanaltest ohnehin spielt (`words.take(2)` → Ball, Haus). Autor 2026-08-06: „für den Test brauche ich nur Ball und Haus."

- **Über die ACs hinaus, bewusst: `Row` → `FlowRow` bei den drei Kanaltasten.** Am Gerät sichtbar geworden, als die Wortliste darunter schrumpfte und den Blick nicht mehr ablenkte: Die dritte Taste („Nur „Haus" rechts") war zu einer **einzeichenbreiten Säule** gequetscht — die Labels tragen die Wortnamen und überlaufen die Bildschirmbreite. Dasselbe Überlaufproblem und dieselbe Abhilfe wie bei den Sprecher-Chips und der Aktionszeile je Eintrag (A53-Befund 2026-08-06). Das Nicht-Ziel „die drei Kanaltest-Tasten bleiben unverändert" zielt auf Funktion und `playTwoWordsPerEar`, nicht auf den Layout-Container; ein verstümmeltes Bedienelement auf genau dem Screen auszuliefern, um den dieser Batch geht, wäre schlechter gewesen.

- **Wie verifiziert:** `./gradlew build` grün. **A53: AC1 vom Autor bestätigt** („Langdruck hat funktioniert") — meine eigenen synthetischen Langdrücke (`input swipe` mit Nulldistanz, dann `input motionevent DOWN/UP`) blieben ohne Wirkung bzw. lieferten leere uiautomator-Dumps; die parallele manuelle Bedienung erklärt das. **AC2 am Screenshot belegt** (nur „Ball" und „Haus" in der Liste), und der Startscreen ohne Dev-Knopf ebenfalls. **Offen: die Sichtprobe des `FlowRow`-Umbruchs** — danach schlief der Bildschirm wiederholt ein und der Benachrichtigungsschirm hing im Fokus; ferngesteuert war nicht mehr sinnvoll weiterzuprüfen. Erwartung: die drei Tasten brechen um, statt dass eine zusammengequetscht wird.

- **Versionen:** 0.27.0 (zurückgebaute Fassung) → 0.28.0 → **0.28.1**. Der Patch-Schritt, weil auf dem Gerät bereits ein 0.28.0 lief, als der `FlowRow`-Fix dazukam — genau der Fall, den die an diesem Tag ergänzte DoD-§6-Regel verhindern soll.

## 2026-08-07 (Opus: **Kanaltest nur noch im Debug-Build — v0.27.0**) — **überholt durch v0.28.1, siehe oben**

- **Dieser Stand ist vollständig zurückgebaut.** Er war an einem geschärften Backlog-Item vorbeigebaut; der Eintrag bleibt stehen, weil die API-Belege darin gültig sind, aber der beschriebene Code existiert nicht mehr.
- **Was:** Autor-Requirement „Kanaltest ausblenden". Der Dev-Kanaltest verschwindet aus Release-Builds, bleibt in Debug-Builds einen Tap entfernt. **Ausgeblendet, nicht gelöscht:** Das Werkzeug hat echten diagnostischen Wert — die ASHA-Volume-State-Beweiskette vom 2026-07-18 kam daher —, gehört aber in keine App, die an Fremde geht (M6/M7).
- **Wie:** `expect fun isDebugBuild()` in `composeApp/commonMain` (`DebugBuild.kt`), Android-Actual liest `BuildConfig.DEBUG`, Desktop-Actual gibt `true` zurück (Dev-Target, wird nicht verteilt — den Diagnose-Knopf ausgerechnet dort zu verstecken wäre verkehrt herum). Dafür `buildFeatures { buildConfig = true }` in `composeApp/build.gradle.kts`; AGP 8 erzeugt `BuildConfig` sonst nicht, und nichts sonst im Projekt liest daraus. Der Startscreen-Knopf steht in einem `if (isDebugBuild())`; `Screen.DevKanaltest` und `DevPlaybackScreen` bleiben verdrahtet — der Weg ist unerreichbar, nicht entfernt.
- **Wie verifiziert:** `./gradlew build` grün. Der Mechanismus ist an den generierten Quellen belegt statt angenommen: `buildConfig/release/…/BuildConfig.java` trägt `DEBUG = false`, die Debug-Variante `DEBUG = Boolean.parseBoolean("true")`. **Sichtprobe am A53 vom Autor bestätigt (2026-08-07): „Kanaltest Button ist sichtbar in 0.27.0"** — Debug-Build, also der erwartete Ausgang; die Gating-Logik hat den Knopf dort nicht versehentlich mitgenommen.

  **Der Release-Fall bleibt unbelegt, und zwar bewusst.** Prüfen ließe er sich nur mit einem installierbaren Release-Build; der ist heute unsigniert (eigenes M7-Item), und ein mit dem Debug-Keystore behelfssignierter Release-Build trüge eine andere Signatur als die installierte App — die Installation verlangte also erst eine **Deinstallation**, und die löscht den Eigen-Korpus samt der einzigen Aufnahme der zweiten Sprecherin. Diesen Preis für den Nachweis eines `if` auf einem `static final boolean` zu zahlen, dessen Wert (`DEBUG = false`) in den generierten Quellen nachgelesen ist, wäre unverhältnismäßig. Fällt ohnehin an, sobald das Signier-Item aus M7 gezogen wird.
- **Nebenher:** Die Versions-Doku in `AppVersion.kt` sagte „Patch bleibt Hotfixes vorbehalten" und widersprach damit der an diesem Tag ergänzten DoD-§6-Regel (auch kurze Fixes zählen die Patch-Stelle hoch). Angeglichen.

## 2026-08-07 (Opus: **Sicherung eigener Aufnahmen: ZIP-Export/Import — und `allowBackup` aus — v0.26.0**)

- **Abnahme am A53 (2026-08-07): bestanden, einschließlich des Tests, der als einziger etwas beweist.** Vollständiger Durchlauf: exportieren → **App deinstallieren** → neu installieren → importieren. Ergebnis am Gerät gemessen, nicht am Bildschirm abgelesen:
